Abstract. In Europe, economic evaluation of flood manage-          1   Introduction ment projects is increasingly used to help decision making. Table 1. Classification of flood damage to agriculture [SOURCE: Page_5]

Instantaneous (just after flooding)                  Induced (later after flooding) Direct (due to flood exposure) – crop loss and yield reduction                     – loss of added value due to the loss of – livestock fatalities                                yield in the first years after replanting perennial plant material (orchard, vine- – loss of livestock products (e.g. milk)              yard) or reseeding grass – damage to perennial plant material                – loss of added value due to unavailability – damage to soil                                      of production factors (machinery, inputs etc.) – damage to buildings – cost of relocation or premature sales of – damage to machinery livestock – damage to stored inputs – cost of additional food for livestock – damage to infrastructure (e.g. roads) – reduction of herd size [SOURCE: Page_5]

To sum up, the studies we reviewed used several economic               Damage to farm buildings is usually evaluated by repair indicators depending on how the variable production costs              and cleaning costs. Concerning damage to machinery, dam- were considered to be impacted by flood events. A really               age is evaluated by the cost of repairing or repurchasing the precise estimate of the variation in variable costs requires           machine. From an economic point of view, the correct value knowing the distribution of production costs over the year.            to be used to evaluate this damage is the depreciated value. If numerous flood events are simulated for several seasons of          Therefore, CA30 (2009) proposes to account for the age of occurrence, the calculations should be automated.                      the pool of machinery when defining a depreciation rate. It is also the assumption made by Brémond (2011) to evaluate damage to machinery. 3.5.2   Damage indicators for the other farm components                   In the studies reviewed which consider damage to plant material, the damage is usually evaluated by replanting costs and loss of added value before the orchard or vineyard reach When material damage to farm components other than crop, their previous production potential. i.e. damage to soil, buildings, machinery, stocks, is evaluated, the economic indicator used is the reparation cost. These reparation costs are evaluated by the cost of actions neces-           4     Analysis sary to recover a status equivalent to the one before the flood event occurred or the costs of repurchasing what was lost.             4.1       Comparing ex-post and ex-ante studies Damage to soil is evaluated by cleaning costs and addi- tional tillage, and gap filling which may have to be imple-            Our review revealed a gap between ex-post studies (Sect. 2) mented depending on flood intensity.
